`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Ouroboros test** (`test_actuator_patch_verification_success`): The test calls `execute_patch` on a real file path, which runs `subprocess.run(["uv", "run", "pytest", "-q", "--tb=no"])` — the *full test suite*. In CI, the suite has 2 failing tests (itself + weight_bridge), so `returncode != 0` → returns `False` → assertion fails. This is a circular dependency: the test can only pass when *all* tests pass. The fix is to mock the subprocess call.

**Weight bridge test** (`test_layer_collapse_correctness`): Matrix multiplication `mean_w @ shared2_w` accumulates float32 rounding errors. One element out of 32,768 exceeds `rtol=1e-3` by ~0.026%. Fix: add `atol=1e-8` to tolerate the tiny absolute difference (`5.8e-09`).
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why these were the last 2 failures:**
- The ouroboros test had a **circular dependency** — it ran the full pytest suite as part of verification, so if *any* test failed, this one would too. Mocking `subprocess.run` breaks the cycle while still testing the patch-verification logic path.
- The weight bridge test hit a **float32 matrix multiplication accumulation boundary** — `mean_w @ shared2_w` over 32,768 elements drifts by ~6e-9 depending on CPU instruction ordering (AVX-512 vs SSE paths). `atol=1e-8` absorbs this while still catching real regressions.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
These are **cloud-vault-mcp** test failures — a separate sub-project. The failing `test (3.13)` is from the "Tests" workflow which runs *both* root project tests AND cloud-vault-mcp tests. The root tests (3,178+ passed) are clean. The cloud-vault-mcp failures are:
- `test_pocket_tts` (5 failures): `TTSModel` attribute missing — likely a renamed/removed class
- `test_sheets_research_daemon` (2 failures): JSON extraction logic mismatch
- `test_vault_ops` (2 failures): Regex pattern for path traversal error messages changed

These are pre-existing issues in the cloud-vault-mcp sub-project, not caused by our changes. Let me fix them to get a fully green CI.

```
     1→"""Pocket TTS integration for text-to-speech synthesis.
     2→
     3→This module provides text-to-speech synthesis using the Pocket TTS model
     4→(100M parameter lightweight model). Follows the token-efficient pattern:
     5→implementation first, then validation, then tests.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→import base64
     9→import io
    10→import logging
    11→from typing import Any
    12→
    13→
    14→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    15→
    16→
    17→class PocketTTSService:
    18→    """Text-to-speech synthesis using Pocket TTS (100M param model)."""
    19→
    20→    def __init__(self) -> None:
    21→        """Initialize service with lazy loading."""
    22→        self.model = None
    23→        self.sample_rate = 24000
    24→        self._initialized = False
    25→
    26→    def initialize(self) -> None:
    27→        """Load TTS model on first use (lazy initialization).
    28→
    29→        Raises:
    30→            RuntimeError: If pocket-tts not installed or model load fails
    31→        """
    32→        if self._initialized:
    33→            return
    34→
    35→        try:
    36→            from pocket_tts import TTSModel
    37→
    38→            self.model = TTSModel.load_model(
    39→                config="b6369a24", temp=0.7, eos_threshold=-4.0
    40→            )
    41→            self.sample_rate = self.model.sample_rate
    42→            self._initialized = True
    43→            logger.info("Pocket TTS model loaded on %s", self.model.device)
    44→
    45→        except ImportError as e:
    46→            raise RuntimeError(
    47→                "pocket-tts not installed: pip install pocket-tts"
    48→            ) from e
    49→        except Exception as e:
    50→            raise RuntimeError(f"Failed to load TTS model: {e}") from e
    51→
    52→    def speak(self, text: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    53→        """Synthesize text to speech.
    54→
    55→        Args:
    56→            text: Text to synthesize (max 4096 chars)
    57→
    58→        Returns:
    59→            Dictionary with:
    60→            - status: "success" or "error"
    61→            - audio_base64: Base64-encoded WAV audio (if success)
    62→            - duration_ms: Audio duration in milliseconds (if success)
    63→            - sample_rate: Sample rate in Hz (if success)
    64→            - model: Model identifier (if success)
    65→            - error: Error message (if error)
    66→        """
    67→        # Validate input first (before expensive model initialization)
    68→        if not text or not text.strip():
    69→            return {"status": "error", "error": "Text cannot be empty"}
    70→
    71→        if len(text) > 4096:
    72→            return {"status": "error", "error": "Text too long (max 4096 chars)"}
    73→
    74→        # Initialize on first use
    75→        if not self._initialized:
    76→            try:
    77→                self.initialize()
    78→            except RuntimeError as e:
    79→                return {"status": "error", "error": str(e)}
    80→
    81→        try:
    82→            import torch
    83→            import torchaudio
    84→
    85→            # Get default voice state (no voice cloning for MVP)
    86→            # Using 1 second of random noise as default voice prompt
    87→            voice_state = self.model.get_state_for_audio_prompt(torch.randn(24000))
    88→
    89→            # Generate audio
    90→            audio_tensor = self.model.generate_audio(voice_state, text, copy_state=True)
    91→
    92→            # Convert to WAV bytes
    93→            buffer = io.BytesIO()
    94→            torchaudio.save(
    95→                buffer,
    96→                audio_tensor.unsqueeze(0),  # Add batch dimension
    97→                self.sample_rate,
    98→                format="wav",
    99→            )
   100→            audio_bytes = buffer.getvalue()
   101→
   102→            # Calculate duration
   103→            duration_ms = int(len(audio_tensor) / self.sample_rate * 1000)
   104→
   105→            return {
   106→                "status": "success",
   107→                "audio_base64": base64.b64encode(audio_bytes).decode(),
   108→                "duration_ms": duration_ms,
   109→                "sample_rate": self.sample_rate,
   110→                "model": "pocket-tts",
   111→            }
   112→
   113→        except Exception as e:
   114→            logger.error("TTS synthesis failed: %s", e)
   115→            return {"status": "error", "error": str(e)}
   116→
```

> AGENT

The TTS tests use `@patch("pocket_tts.TTSModel")` which patches the module-level attribute — but `pocket_tts` is an external package. The `PocketTTSService.initialize()` does `from pocket_tts import TTSModel` at runtime. The `@patch` decorator is patching the module attribute, but when `pocket_tts` isn't installed in CI, the import fails before the patch takes effect.

Let me check the other failures too.

> AGENT

Now I see the issues clearly:

1. **vault_ops**: The error message for `../../etc/passwd` is `"Invalid path (traversal attempt)"` (line 35), not `"escapes vault"` (line 52). The test expects `match="escapes vault"` but the `..` check triggers first with a different message.

2. **sheets_research_daemon**: The test constructs JSONL output with escaped JSON blocks — the regex `r"```json\s*([\s\S]*?)\s*```"` can't find `\`\`\`json` in the escaped `text` field of a JSONL wrapper. The JSON blocks are double-escaped within the JSONL delta format.

3. **pocket_tts**: `@patch("pocket_tts.TTSModel")` fails when `pocket_tts` isn't installed — the patch target module doesn't exist.

Let me fix all three. The most elegant approach: fix the tests to match the actual behavior.

> TOOL

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The `pocket_tts` module is an optional dependency — it's imported inside `initialize()` via `from pocket_tts import TTSModel`. When the package isn't installed (CI), `@patch("pocket_tts.TTSModel")` fails because Python can't find the `pocket_tts` module to patch its attribute. The fix is to ensure the module exists before patching by inserting a mock module into `sys.modules`. This is a standard pattern for testing optional dependency imports.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_019aTwQ42P5HJwQ7pWwg6RWi
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/cloud-vault-mcp/tests/test_pocket_tts.py",
  "old_string": "\"\"\"Tests for Pocket TTS integration.\n\nFollowing token-efficient pattern: tests written AFTER implementation and validation.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nfrom unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch\n\nimport pytest\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef mock_tts_model():\n    \"\"\"Mock TTSModel for testing without real model.\"\"\"\n    import torch\n\n    mock_model = MagicMock()\n    mock_model.device = \"cpu\"\n    mock_model.sample_rate = 24000\n    mock_model.get_state_for_audio_prompt.return_value = {\"state\": \"mock\"}\n    mock_model.generate_audio.return_value = torch.zeros(24000)  # 1 sec silence\n\n    return mock_model",
  "new_string": "\"\"\"Tests for Pocket TTS integration.\n\nFollowing token-efficient pattern: tests written AFTER implementation and validation.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch\n\nimport pytest\n\n# Ensure pocket_tts module exists for patching (optional dependency)\nif \"pocket_tts\" not in sys.modules:\n    _mock_pocket_tts = MagicMock()\n    sys.modules[\"pocket_tts\"] = _mock_pocket_tts\n\n\n@pytest.fixture\ndef mock_tts_model():\n    \"\"\"Mock TTSModel for testing without real model.\"\"\"\n    import torch\n\n    mock_model = MagicMock()\n    mock_model.device = \"cpu\"\n    mock_model.sample_rate = 24000\n    mock_model.get_state_for_audio_prompt.return_value = {\"state\": \"mock\"}\n    mock_model.generate_audio.return_value = torch.zeros(24000)  # 1 sec silence\n\n    return mock_model"
}
```

> AGENT

The problem: the test passes JSONL-wrapped output where ` ```json ` and `\"` are escaped *inside* a JSON string. The `extract_json_from_output` method uses regex `r"```json\s*([\s\S]*?)\s*```"` which looks for literal triple-backticks — but in the test string, the backticks are inside the `"text"` field of a JSON wrapper, where `\\n` is the literal two characters `\n`, not a newline.

The regex operates on the raw string — it needs the actual characters `` ```json `` followed by content followed by `` ``` ``, but the test data has these inside JSON escape sequences. The test data is malformed for what the function actually expects.

The fix: the tests should pass plain text with actual `` ```json `` blocks, not JSONL-wrapped output — unless the function is designed to extract from JSONL. Let me check the function more carefully.

> TOOL

```
    21→@pytest.mark.asyncio

    24→    async with GraphRAGQuery() as query:
```

```
     1→"""Tests for GraphRAG hybrid query"""
     2→
     3→
     4→import pytest
     5→
     6→from mcp_server.graphrag_query import GraphRAGQuery, _cache_key
     7→
     8→
     9→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    10→async def test_semantic_search():
    11→    """Test semantic vector search"""
    12→    async with GraphRAGQuery() as query:
    13→        results = await query.semantic_search("test query", top_k=3)
    14→        assert isinstance(results, list)
    15→        # Should return results if vault has documents
    16→        if results:
    17→            assert "id" in results[0]
    18→            assert "score" in results[0]
    19→
    20→
    21→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    22→async def test_hybrid_search():
    23→    """Test hybrid semantic + graph search"""
    24→    async with GraphRAGQuery() as query:
    25→        results = await query.hybrid_search("test query", top_k=3)
    26→        assert isinstance(results, list)
    27→        # Should have graph fields even if empty
    28→        if results:
    29→            doc = results[0]
    30→            assert "id" in doc
    31→            assert "score" in doc
    32→            # ancestors/descendants may be None if no edges
    33→            assert "ancestors" in doc or "descendants" in doc
    34→
    35→
    36→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    37→async def test_find_related():
    38→    """Test finding related documents via graph"""
    39→    async with GraphRAGQuery() as query:
    40→        # Use a document we know exists
    41→        result = await query.find_related("vault_memory:template")
    42→        assert isinstance(result, dict)
    43→        # Should have relationship fields
    44→        assert "id" in result or not result  # Empty if doc doesn't exist
    45→
    46→
    47→def test_cache_key():
    48→    """Test cache key generation"""
    49→    key1 = _cache_key("query1", 5, True, False)
    50→    key2 = _cache_key("query1", 5, True, False)
    51→    key3 = _cache_key("query2", 5, True, False)
    52→
    53→    assert key1 == key2  # Same params = same key
    54→    assert key1 != key3  # Different query = different key
    55→
    56→
    57→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    58→async def test_embedding_generation():
    59→    """Test query embedding generation"""
    60→    async with GraphRAGQuery() as query:
    61→        embedding = await query.generate_embedding("test text")
    62→        assert isinstance(embedding, list)
    63→        assert len(embedding) == 768  # nomic-embed-text dimension
    64→
    65→
    66→@pytest.mark.asyncio
    67→async def test_min_score_filter():
    68→    """Test minimum score filtering"""
    69→    async with GraphRAGQuery() as query:
    70→        # High min_score should return fewer results
    71→        results_high = await query.semantic_search("test", top_k=10, min_score=0.8)
    72→        results_low = await query.semantic_search("test", top_k=10, min_score=0.1)
    73→
    74→        assert len(results_high) <= len(results_low)
    75→
```

**What we fixed across this session (7 commits):**
1. CodeQL security alerts — centralized `safe_input.py` utility for path traversal + log injection
2. GitHub Actions workflows — `paths` filters, existence checks, `npm install` vs `npm ci`
3. `test_isolation.py` — `unittest.skipIf` for OverlayFS in CI (pytestmark doesn't work on TestCase)
4. `test_ouroboros_loop.py` — mocked subprocess to break circular test dependency
5. `test_weight_bridge.py` — `atol=1e-8` for float32 matrix multiplication tolerance
6. `test_pocket_tts.py` — mock module in `sys.modules` for optional dependency
7. `test_vault_ops.py`, `test_sheets_research_daemon.py` — regex match + test data format fixes
8. Integration tests — CI skip for tests requiring Ollama/SurrealDB/local vault
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

The platform implements a 12-dimensional axiomatic state manifold where autonomous agents (modeled as Exotic Vacuum Objects, or EVOs) navigate semantic space under physics-grounded constraints. The central stability mechanism, the **HIHO Principle** (Half-In, Half-Out), enforces a Hooke's Law attractor at 0.5 coherence, mathematically preventing both hallucination collapse (coherence -> 0) and rigid over-fitting (coherence -> 1).
     6→
     7→The system comprises 444 Python modules (107K LOC), a Rust physics core for SIMD-accelerated computations, a Next.js visualization dashboard, and a full SDLC methodology with 7 epics, 55 stories, and bidirectional requirement traceability.
     8→
     9→---
    10→
    11→## 1. The HIHO Principle
    12→
    13→The HIHO (Half-In, Half-Out) Principle defines a restoring force that drives agent coherence toward a stable equilibrium at 0.5, analogous to Hooke's Law in classical mechanics:
    14→
    15→```
    16→F_restore = k * (0.5 - C(t)) * dt
    17→```
    18→
    19→where `C(t)` is the agent's coherence at time `t`, `k = 2.0` is the spring constant, and `dt` is the simulation time step.
    20→
    21→**Implementation** (`hiho_unified_engine.py:192-212`):
    22→```python
    23→delta_coherence = 0.5 - evo.coherence
    24→restoring_force = 2.0 * delta_coherence * dt
    25→evo.coherence += restoring_force
    26→
    27→# Rapid decay when coherence deviates beyond safe boundary
    28→if abs(evo.coherence - 0.5) > 0.4:
    29→    vec *= np.exp(-dt * 5.0)
    30→```
    31→
    32→This creates a damped harmonic oscillator around the 0.5 boundary: agents that drift too far toward either extreme experience exponential state decay, forcing self-correction. The 0.5 equilibrium represents optimal balance between exploitation (high coherence, deterministic) and exploration (low coherence, stochastic).
    33→
    34→---
    35→
    36→## 2. Exotic Vacuum Objects (EVOs)
    37→
    38→Agents are modeled as EVO charge clusters with computable physical properties (`hiho_unified_engine.py:117-128`):
    39→
    40→| Property | Type | Physical Meaning |
    41→|----------|------|-----------------|
    42→| `charge_density` | float | Agent activation energy |
    43→| `magnetic_helicity` | float [-0.5, 0.5] | Topological twist (chirality parameter) |
    44→| `toroidal_moment` | float [0.5, 2.0] | Fractal self-similar scaling (breathing mode) |
    45→| `coherence` | float [0, 1] | HIHO stability state (initialized at 0.5) |
    46→
    47→EVOs are initialized via `EVOInitializationFactory.create_evo()` at the HIHO boundary (`coherence=0.5`) and evolve under the combined influence of:
    48→
    49→1. **MHD Helicity Rotation** (`hiho_unified_engine.py:131-171`): Rotates the first two latent dimensions by an angle proportional to `magnetic_helicity * dt`, preserving topological invariants.
    50→2. **Toroidal Breathing Mode**: Scales the state vector toward the attractor defined by `toroidal_moment`, with `scale_factor = 1.0 + (toroidal_moment - ||vec||) * 0.1 * dt`.
    51→3. **Charge Dissipation**: `charge_density *= exp(-dt * (1 - coherence))` — coherent agents retain charge; incoherent agents dissipate.
    52→
    53→---
    54→
    55→## 3. 12-Dimensional Axiomatic State
    56→
    57→The `AxiomaticState` (`engine.py:33-97`) maps agent state to a 12D manifold where each dimension carries semantic meaning:
    58→
    59→| Dim | Name | Range | Semantic |
    60→|-----|------|-------|----------|
    61→| 0 | Logic | [-1, 1] | Deductive reasoning strength |
    62→| 1 | Intuition | [-1, 1] | Abductive/creative capacity |
    63→| 2 | Coherence | [0, 1] | HIHO stability metric |
    64→| 3 | Ethics | [0, 1] | Constitutional alignment |
    65→| 4 | Complexity | [0, inf) | State information content |
    66→| 5 | Time | [0, inf) | Temporal position |
    67→| 6-11 | Extended | [-1, 1] | Domain-specific axes |
    68→
    69→The FLUME pipeline (Fluid Latent Understanding through Manifold Encoding) compresses raw observations through a VAE: `2048D -> 256D latent -> 12D axiomatic` (`vae_encoder.py:33-60`).
    70→
    71→---
    72→
    73→## 4. Physics Engines
    74→
    75→The `HIHOUnifiedEngine` (`hiho_unified_engine.py:459-586`) orchestrates nine physics sub-engines per simulation tick:
    76→
    77→| Engine | Physics | Code Reference |
    78→|--------|---------|---------------|
    79→| CellularAutomata | Wolfram Rule 30, 256-cell grid, toroidal topology | `hiho_unified_engine.py:41-84` |
    80→| ChaosTheory | Butterfly effect: \|dZ(t)\| = \|dZ(0)\| * e^(lambda*t) | `hiho_unified_engine.py:97-114` |
    81→| MHD | Helicity rotation + toroidal breathing mode | `hiho_unified_engine.py:131-171` |
    82→| HIHO Stabilization | Hooke's Law restoring force at C=0.5 | `hiho_unified_engine.py:189-212` |
    83→| Penrose Twistors | CP^3 twistor space mapping (C4 spinor rotation) | `hiho_unified_engine.py:237-255` |
    84→| Quantum Emergence | ER=EPR entanglement, Planck quantization, chirality | `hiho_unified_engine.py:258-276` |
    85→| Bioelectrics | Orch-OR microtubule alignment, morphogenetic fields | `hiho_unified_engine.py:279-306` |
    86→| Sacred Geometry | Torus alignment metric (major_R=2.0, minor_r=0.5) | `hiho_unified_engine.py:215-234` |
    87→| Kordylewski Swarm | L4/L5 Lagrange point orbital mechanics | `hiho_unified_engine.py:328-389` |
    88→
    89→A Rust physics core (`src/cohezion-physics-core/src/lib.rs`) provides SIMD-accelerated implementations of CA evolution and MHD force application via PyO3 bindings, with automatic Python fallback when the native module is unavailable.
    90→
    91→---

    93→## 5. RL Training Environment
    94→
    95→`FlumeNavEnv` (`rl/environment.py:23-100`) implements a Gymnasium environment:
    96→
    97→- **Observation space**: `Box(-inf, inf, shape=(256,))` — FLUME latent vectors
    98→- **Action space**: `Box(-1, 1, shape=(256,))` — continuous perturbations to the latent state
    99→- **Reward shaping** (`reward_shaping.py`):
   100→  - `CoherenceReward`: Gaussian centered at 0.5 with sigma=0.2 — `R = exp(-(C - 0.5)^2 / (2 * 0.2^2))`
   101→  - `DiversityBonus`: Rewards exploration of novel latent regions
   102→  - `CompositeReward`: Weighted combination for multi-objective optimization
   103→
   104→---
   105→
   106→## 6. Safety Architecture
   107→
   108→### Constitutional Governance
   109→`.agent/CONSTITUTION.md` implements Anthropic's safety hierarchy: **Safe > Ethical > Compliant > Helpful**. All agent outputs are validated against constitutional constraints before execution.
   110→
   111→### Eval-Awareness Defense
   112→`security/eval_awareness_defense.py` injects canary tokens into agent reasoning traces and monitors for metacognitive patterns that indicate evaluation-aware behavior modification.
   113→
   114→### Ethical Framework
   115→`security/ethical_framework.py` implements a 4-principle hierarchy addressing OWASP agentic threat categories. All simulation data is subject to `ConsentManager` validation with SHA-256 signed consent tokens.
   116→
   117→### Sandbox Isolation
   118→`universe/sandbox_manager.py` enforces an 85GB system memory budget across all simulation sandboxes, with automatic backend selection (Docker, subprocess, or in-process) and per-sandbox divergence detection.
   119→
   120→---
   121→
   122→## 7. Compound Engineering Pipeline
   123→
   124→The 11-step execution pipeline (`compound/executor.py`) implements a self-improving loop:
   125→
   126→```
   127→PRIME Skill -> InstructionExpander -> PlanExecutor -> ExecutionOrchestrator
   128→  -> RequestAlignmentAnalyzer (coherence check)
   129→  -> GlobalMetricsAggregator (record metrics)

   133→  -> SkillRefiner (update skill definition)
   134→  -> SkillConsensusVoter (multi-agent validation)
   135→  -> Updated Skill (loop)
   136→```
   137→
   138→Cost optimization via `CostAwareRouter` achieves 27.3% savings by routing 90% of routine operations through local SLMs (Ollama: deepseek-r1:70b, qwen3-coder:30b, phi3:mini). `SemanticCache` (L1 hash + L2 cosine + L3 vault) maintains 95%+ hit rate.

   165→- **PRD**: 24 functional requirements, 15 non-functional requirements

```
     1→"""WebMCP Bridge - Routes browser tool calls to real MCP server handlers.
     2→
     3→Connects the browser-based AG-UI to the MCP fleet by looking up servers
     4→in the registry and dispatching tool calls to their Python handlers.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→import asyncio
    10→import logging
    11→import re
    12→from typing import Any
    13→
    14→import httpx
    15→from aiohttp import web
    16→
    17→from cohezion.mcp.registry import get_registry
    18→
    19→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    20→
    21→# Allowed name pattern: alphanumeric, hyphens, underscores; max 64 chars
    22→_NAME_RE = re.compile(r"^[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9_-]{0,63}$")
    23→
    24→
    25→class WebMCPBridge:
    26→    """Routes browser MCP tool calls to real server handlers."""
    27→
    28→    def __init__(self, port: int = 8380) -> None:
    29→        self.port = port
    30→        self.registry = get_registry()
    31→        self.app = web.Application()
    32→        self._setup_routes()
    33→
    34→    def _setup_routes(self) -> None:
    35→        self.app.add_routes([
    36→            web.get("/.well-known/model-context.json", self.handle_context),
    37→            web.get("/mcp/list_servers", self.handle_list_servers),
    38→            web.post("/mcp/call_tool", self.handle_call_tool),
    39→        ])
    40→
    41→    @staticmethod
    42→    def _validate_name(name: str) -> bool:
    43→        """Validate server/tool names to prevent injection attacks."""
    44→        if not name:
    45→            return False
    46→        return bool(_NAME_RE.match(name))
    47→
    48→    async def handle_context(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    49→        """Returns the model-context.json for MCP discovery."""
    50→        servers = self.registry.list_servers()
    51→        context = {
    52→            "mcpServers": {
    53→                s.name: {
    54→                    "url": s.url if s.url else f"http://localhost:{8360 + servers.index(s)}",
    55→                    "description": s.description,
    56→                }
    57→                for s in servers
    58→                if s.status == "available" or s.url
    59→            }
    60→        }
    61→        return web.json_response(context)
    62→
    63→    async def handle_list_servers(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    64→        servers = self.registry.list_servers()
    65→        return web.json_response([s.to_dict() for s in servers])
    66→
    67→    async def handle_call_tool(self, request: web.Request) -> web.Response:
    68→        """Route a tool call to the actual MCP server handler."""
    69→        server_name = ""
    70→        try:
    71→            data = await request.json()
    72→            server_name = str(data.get("server", ""))
    73→            tool_name = str(data.get("tool", ""))
    74→            arguments: dict[str, Any] = data.get("arguments", {})
    75→
    76→            # Validate names
    77→            if not self._validate_name(server_name):
    78→                return web.json_response(
    79→                    {"success": False, "error": f"Invalid server name: {server_name!r}"},
    80→                    status=400,
    81→                )
    82→            if not self._validate_name(tool_name):
    83→                return web.json_response(
    84→                    {"success": False, "error": f"Invalid tool name: {tool_name!r}"},
    85→                    status=400,
    86→                )
    87→
    88→            logger.info("WebMCP Call: %s.%s(%s)", server_name, tool_name, arguments)
    89→
    90→            # Look up server in registry
    91→            server = self.registry.get_server(server_name)
    92→            if server is None:
    93→                return web.json_response(
    94→                    {"success": False, "error": f"Server not found: {server_name}"},
    95→                    status=404,
    96→                )
    97→
    98→            # Route to the server's HTTP endpoint if it has a URL
    99→            if server.url:
   100→                result = await self._call_http_server(server.url, tool_name, arguments)
   101→            else:
   102→                # Internal server without URL — call via local module dispatch
   103→                result = await self._call_internal_server(server_name, tool_name, arguments)
   104→
   105→            return web.json_response({"success": True, "result": result, "server": server_name})
   106→
   107→        except httpx.ConnectError:
   108→            return web.json_response(
   109→                {"success": False, "error": f"Server {server_name} is offline"},
   110→                status=503,
   111→            )
   112→        except Exception as e:
   113→            logger.exception("WebMCP call failed")
   114→            return web.json_response({"success": False, "error": str(e)}, status=500)
   115→
   116→    async def _call_http_server(
   117→        self, base_url: str, tool_name: str, arguments: dict[str, Any]
   118→    ) -> Any:
   119→        """Call an HTTP-based MCP server."""
   120→        async with httpx.AsyncClient(timeout=10.0) as client:
   121→            resp = await client.post(
   122→                f"{base_url}/tools/{tool_name}",
   123→                json=arguments,
   124→            )
   125→            resp.raise_for_status()
   126→            return resp.json()
   127→
   128→    async def _call_internal_server(
   129→        self, server_name: str, tool_name: str, arguments: dict[str, Any]
   130→    ) -> Any:
   131→        """Dispatch to a local Python MCP server handler."""
   132→        # Lazy-import server modules to avoid circular imports
   133→        handler = self._resolve_internal_handler(server_name, tool_name)
   134→        if handler is None:
   135→            return {"error": f"No handler for {server_name}.{tool_name}"}
   136→
   137→        if asyncio.iscoroutinefunction(handler):
   138→            return await handler(**arguments)
   139→        return handler(**arguments)
   140→
   141→    def _resolve_internal_handler(
   142→        self, server_name: str, tool_name: str
   143→    ) -> Any | None:
   144→        """Resolve a Python handler function for an internal MCP tool."""
   145→        # Map server names to their handler modules
   146→        try:
   147→            if server_name == "knowledge":
   148→                from cohezion.mcp.knowledge_server import get_server
   149→
   150→                srv = get_server()
   151→                return getattr(srv, tool_name, None)
   152→            if server_name == "swarm":
   153→                from cohezion.mcp.swarm_server import get_server
   154→
   155→                srv = get_server()
   156→                return getattr(srv, tool_name, None)
   157→        except Exception:
   158→            logger.warning("Failed to resolve handler for %s.%s", server_name, tool_name)
   159→        return None
   160→
   161→    async def start(self) -> None:
   162→        runner = web.AppRunner(self.app)
   163→        await runner.setup()
   164→        site = web.TCPSite(runner, "0.0.0.0", self.port)
   165→        await site.start()
   166→        logger.info("WebMCP Bridge started on port %d", self.port)
   167→
   168→
   169→if __name__ == "__main__":
   170→    logging.basicConfig(level=logging.INFO)
   171→    bridge = WebMCPBridge()
   172→    loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   173→    loop.run_until_complete(bridge.start())
   174→    loop.run_forever()
   175→
```

```
     5→
     6→# cohezion - Epic Breakdown (Bidirectional Architecture)
     7→
     8→## Overview
     9→
    10→This document provides the complete epic and story breakdown for cohezion, decomposing the requirements from the PRD, UX Design, and Architecture requirements into implementable stories.
    11→
    12→## Requirements Inventory
    13→
    14→### Functional Requirements
    15→
    16→FR1: [12D State Tracking] The system shall record and persist agent thought trajectories as 12D coordinate arrays in SurrealDB at <10ms latency.
    17→FR2: [512D Projection] The `FlumeEncoder` shall compress 512D semantic vectors into 12D axiomatic vectors for physics engine processing.
    18→FR3: [VLIW Alignment] The Rust bridge (`flume_physics.rs`) shall execute latent instruction packets using barrier-locked, VLEN=8 SIMD alignment.
    19→FR4: [Multilingual Audio Bridge] The system shall stream real-time audio representations of agent state using the Kyutai `mimi` codec and `moshi-v1` / `hibiki-v1` models.
    20→FR5: [Vanguard Scouting] The Daily Vanguard Agent shall scrape ArXiv, HuggingFace, GitHub, Reddit, and Ollama to extract SLM fine-tuning scripts and SOTA abstractions, executing automated integration tests with strict attribution and license compliance.
    21→FR6: [Metacognitive Auditing] The system shall require every agent to log a JSON payload explaining its 512D intent prior to executing a 12D physical state change.
    22→FR7: [Autonomous Test Generation] The `ShadowScripter` agent shall autonomously generate and commit new test specifications in response to VAE fine-tuning events or Vanguard Scout discoveries.
    23→FR8: [Semantic Memory Pruning] The system shall implement semantic decay and vector pruning for the 512D latent space, ensuring that low-relevance agentic experiences are archived to SurrealDB.
    24→FR9: [Hallucination Truth Anchors] The system shall utilize hardware-native telemetry (Physical Truth Anchors) to ground 512D reasoning, triggering an automatic "Reality Check" if agentic output contradicts the physical substrate state.
    25→FR10: [Token Optimization] The system shall employ a `CostAwareRouter` achieving a 96.25% MoE optimization, prioritizing local SLMs for 90% of tasks and routing only high-complexity synthesis to elite models.
    26→FR11: [Compound Skill Extraction] The system shall perform a daily "Mycelium Audit" of the `MISSION_JOURNAL` and `KEY_LEARNINGS`, autonomously synthesizing and registering new reusable skills in the `src/cohezion/registry/`.
    27→FR12: [Triune Navigation] The system shall implement three distinct cognitive modes: KNOWER (Observatory), THINKER (Vault), and DOER (Cockpit) with embodied transitions (400ms-800ms).
    28→FR13: [Re-Entry Narrative] The system shall present a first-person "Re-Entry Narrative" on session arrival, orienting the user to recent compound growth and system state.
    29→FR14: [HIHO-Reactive UI] The interface shall dynamically respond to the HIHO coherence value, shifting color (Biolume to Orbital) and particle density via a live CSS bridge.
    30→FR15: [Anima Sigil] The system shall feature an edge-native multimodal voice (Gemma 3n) for real-time narration, orientation, and reflection.
    31→FR16: [Provenance-First UX] Every live data point and vault result shall carry a hover-accessible `ProvenanceTag` tracing it to its physical or semantic source.
    32→FR17: [Semantic Vault Search] The THINKER mode shall provide natural language semantic search across the Three Pillars (Decisions, Experiments, Patterns) with visible match reasoning.
    33→FR18: [Loop Visualization] The DOER mode shall provide high-fidelity visualization of the compound cycle, including retrospection summaries and before/after skill diffs.
    34→FR19: [Lifecycle Pre-Simulation] The system shall support "Pre-Precipitation Simulation" where agents model the 12D trajectory of a proposed implementation lifecycle to detect architectural collisions.
    35→FR20: [Adversarial Grounding] The system shall perform periodic "Adversarial Reality Checks" by injecting external, non-agentic "Truth Anchors" to detect and pop "Coherence Bubbles."
    36→FR22: [Persistent Homology Validation] The system shall use Persistent Homology to validate the topological persistence of semantic intent during 12D projection.
    37→FR23: [Hardware-Isolated Trust] All cryptographic signing keys shall be stored in a hardware-isolated environment (TEE/Secure Enclave).
    38→FR24: [Heterogeneous Sharding] The sharding protocol shall support heterogeneous local compute nodes (CPU/GPU/TPU).
    39→
    40→### NonFunctional Requirements
    41→
```

```
     1→"""Divergence detection for sandboxed simulations.
     2→
     3→Monitors per-sandbox coherence, numerical stability, and statistical outliers.
     4→Mirrors the AxiomaticState.coherence_score() pattern from engine.py for HIHO
     5→stability tracking at the 0.5 equilibrium point.
     6→"""
     7→
     8→from __future__ import annotations
     9→
    10→import logging
    11→import math
    12→from collections import deque
    13→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    14→
    15→
    16→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    17→
    18→# HIHO target coherence (Half-In-Half-Out equilibrium)
    19→HIHO_TARGET = 0.5
    20→# Maximum acceptable drift from HIHO target
    21→HIHO_MAX_DRIFT = 0.4
    22→
    23→
    24→@dataclass
    25→class DivergenceStatus:
    26→    """Result of a divergence check.
    27→
    28→    Parameters
    29→    ----------
    30→    diverged : bool
    31→        Whether divergence has been detected.
    32→    reason : str
    33→        Human-readable explanation of divergence (empty if not diverged).
    34→    coherence : float
    35→        Current HIHO coherence score (1.0 = perfect stability at 0.5).
    36→    last_values : list[float]
    37→        Recent values from the monitored signal.
    38→    """
    39→
    40→    diverged: bool
    41→    reason: str
    42→    coherence: float
    43→    last_values: list[float] = field(default_factory=list)
    44→
    45→
    46→class DivergenceDetector:
    47→    """Per-sandbox divergence detector.
    48→
    49→    Detects three classes of divergence:
    50→    1. **NaN/Inf**: Non-finite values in simulation output.
    51→    2. **Statistical outlier**: Value exceeds N standard deviations from running mean.
    52→    3. **HIHO coherence drift**: Coherence drifts >0.4 from the 0.5 target.
    53→
    54→    Parameters
    55→    ----------
    56→    max_sigma : float
    57→        Maximum standard deviations from running mean before flagging divergence.
    58→    window_size : int
    59→        Number of recent values to retain for statistics.
    60→    """
    61→
    62→    def __init__(self, max_sigma: float = 3.0, window_size: int = 100):
    63→        self.max_sigma = max_sigma
    64→        self.window_size = window_size
    65→        self._values: deque[float] = deque(maxlen=window_size)
    66→        self._coherence_values: deque[float] = deque(maxlen=window_size)
    67→        self._sum: float = 0.0
    68→        self._sum_sq: float = 0.0
    69→        self._count: int = 0
    70→
    71→    def check(self, value: float, coherence: float | None = None) -> DivergenceStatus:
    72→        """Check a new value for divergence.
    73→
    74→        Parameters
    75→        ----------
    76→        value : float
    77→            The simulation output value to check.
    78→        coherence : float, optional
    79→            Current HIHO coherence score. If provided, checked for drift.
    80→
    81→        Returns
    82→        -------
    83→        DivergenceStatus
    84→            Whether divergence was detected and why.
    85→        """
    86→        # 1. NaN/Inf check
    87→        if math.isnan(value) or math.isinf(value):
    88→            return DivergenceStatus(
    89→                diverged=True,
    90→                reason=f"Non-finite value detected: {value}",
    91→                coherence=coherence if coherence is not None else 0.0,
    92→                last_values=list(self._values),
    93→            )
    94→
    95→        # 2. Statistical outlier check (only after enough samples)
    96→        if self._count >= 2:
    97→            mean = self._sum / self._count
    98→            variance = (self._sum_sq / self._count) - (mean * mean)
    99→            stddev = math.sqrt(max(variance, 0.0))
   100→
   101→            if stddev > 0:
   102→                z_score = abs(value - mean) / stddev
   103→                if z_score > self.max_sigma:
   104→                    return DivergenceStatus(
   105→                        diverged=True,
   106→                        reason=(
   107→                            f"Statistical outlier: z={z_score:.2f} "
   108→                            f"(>{self.max_sigma}σ), value={value:.6f}, "
   109→                            f"mean={mean:.6f}, stddev={stddev:.6f}"
   110→                        ),
   111→                        coherence=coherence if coherence is not None else 0.0,
   112→                        last_values=list(self._values),
   113→                    )
   114→            elif abs(value - mean) > 0:
   115→                # Zero stddev but value differs from mean — definite outlier
   116→                return DivergenceStatus(
   117→                    diverged=True,
   118→                    reason=(
   119→                        f"Statistical outlier: value={value:.6f} deviates from "
   120→                        f"constant mean={mean:.6f} (stddev=0)"
   121→                    ),
   122→                    coherence=coherence if coherence is not None else 0.0,
   123→                    last_values=list(self._values),
   124→                )
   125→
   126→        # Update running statistics
   127→        self._values.append(value)
   128→        self._sum += value
   129→        self._sum_sq += value * value
   130→        self._count += 1
   131→
   132→        # Recompute running stats periodically when window is full
   133→        if self._count > self.window_size and self._count % self.window_size == 0:
   134→            self._recompute_stats()
   135→
   136→        # 3. HIHO coherence drift check
   137→        current_coherence = coherence if coherence is not None else self._estimate_coherence()
   138→        if coherence is not None:
   139→            self._coherence_values.append(coherence)
   140→
   141→        if abs(current_coherence - HIHO_TARGET) > HIHO_MAX_DRIFT:
   142→            return DivergenceStatus(
   143→                diverged=True,
   144→                reason=(
   145→                    f"HIHO coherence drift: coherence={current_coherence:.3f}, "
   146→                    f"target={HIHO_TARGET}, drift={abs(current_coherence - HIHO_TARGET):.3f} "
   147→                    f"(>{HIHO_MAX_DRIFT})"
   148→                ),
   149→                coherence=current_coherence,
   150→                last_values=list(self._values),
   151→            )
   152→
   153→        return DivergenceStatus(
   154→            diverged=False,
   155→            reason="",
   156→            coherence=current_coherence,
   157→            last_values=list(self._values)[-5:],
   158→        )
   159→
   160→    def _estimate_coherence(self) -> float:
   161→        """Estimate coherence from value stability (mirrors AxiomaticState.coherence_score).
   162→
   163→        Coherence is 1.0 when variance is zero, 0.0 when variance >= 0.25.
   164→        """
   165→        if self._count < 2:
   166→            return HIHO_TARGET
   167→
   168→        mean = self._sum / self._count
   169→        variance = (self._sum_sq / self._count) - (mean * mean)
   170→        variance = max(variance, 0.0)
   171→        return max(1.0 - min(variance * 4, 1.0), 0.0)
   172→
   173→    def _recompute_stats(self) -> None:
   174→        """Recompute running statistics from current window."""
   175→        self._sum = sum(self._values)
   176→        self._sum_sq = sum(v * v for v in self._values)
   177→        self._count = len(self._values)
   178→
   179→    def reset(self) -> None:
   180→        """Reset all state."""
   181→        self._values.clear()
   182→        self._coherence_values.clear()
   183→        self._sum = 0.0
   184→        self._sum_sq = 0.0
   185→        self._count = 0
   186→
   187→    def get_stats(self) -> dict:
   188→        """Return current detector statistics."""
   189→        mean = self._sum / self._count if self._count > 0 else 0.0
   190→        variance = (self._sum_sq / self._count) - (mean * mean) if self._count > 0 else 0.0
   191→        return {
   192→            "count": self._count,
   193→            "mean": mean,
   194→            "variance": max(variance, 0.0),
   195→            "window_size": self.window_size,
   196→            "max_sigma": self.max_sigma,
   197→            "coherence": self._estimate_coherence(),
   198→        }
   199→
```

```
   139→    ) -> np.ndarray:
   140→        """Apply MHD physics to evolve an EVO's latent state vector."""
   141→        vec = latent_vector.copy()
   142→
   143→        if RUST_CORE_AVAILABLE:
   144→            cohezion_physics_core.apply_mhd_forces_simd(
   145→                vec, evo.magnetic_helicity, evo.toroidal_moment, dt
   146→            )
   147→            evo.charge_density *= np.exp(-dt * (1.0 - evo.coherence))
   148→            return vec
   149→
   150→        # Helicity acts as a topological invariant, twisting the vector
   151→        twist_angle = evo.magnetic_helicity * dt
   152→
   153→        # Simplified 12D rotation based on twist (acting on first 2 dims)
   154→        if vec.shape[0] >= 2:
   155→            cos_theta = np.cos(twist_angle)
   156→            sin_theta = np.sin(twist_angle)
   157→            v0, v1 = vec[0].copy(), vec[1].copy()
   158→            vec[0] = v0 * cos_theta - v1 * sin_theta
   159→            vec[1] = v0 * sin_theta + v1 * cos_theta
   160→
   161→        # Toroidal moment defines self-similar scaling (breathing mode)
   162→        # Pulls vector toward a stable attractor defined by toroidal_moment
   163→        current_norm = float(np.linalg.norm(vec))
   164→        if current_norm > 0:
   165→            scale_factor = 1.0 + (evo.toroidal_moment - current_norm) * 0.1 * dt
   166→            vec *= scale_factor
   167→
   168→        # Coherence limits charge dissipation
   169→        evo.charge_density *= np.exp(-dt * (1.0 - evo.coherence))
   170→
   171→        return vec
   172→
   173→
   174→class EVOInitializationFactory:
   175→    """Initializes EVOs using Fractal Toroidal Moments and Plasma Physics."""
   176→
   177→    @staticmethod
   178→    def create_evo(seed: int = 42) -> EvoState:
   179→        """Create an EVO at the origin of Cosmogony."""
   180→        np.random.seed(seed)
   181→        return EvoState(
   182→            charge_density=float(np.random.uniform(0.8, 1.2)),
   183→            magnetic_helicity=float(np.random.uniform(-0.5, 0.5)),  # Chirality parameter
   184→            toroidal_moment=float(np.random.uniform(0.5, 2.0)),
   185→            coherence=0.5,  # Initializes strictly at HIHO boundary
   186→        )
   187→
   188→
   189→class HIHOStabilizationEngine:
   190→    """Enforces the 0.5 Coherence HIHO Principle stabilization loops."""
   191→
   192→    def apply_hiho_loop(
   193→        self,
   194→        evo: EvoState,
   195→        latent_vector: np.ndarray,
   196→        dt: float,
   197→    ) -> tuple[EvoState, np.ndarray]:
   198→        """Drives the EVO back toward the 0.5 coherence boundary."""
   199→        vec = latent_vector.copy()
   200→
   201→        # Distance from the stable 0.5 point
   202→        delta_coherence = 0.5 - evo.coherence
   203→
   204→        # Restoring force (Hooke's law analog for Coherence)
   205→        restoring_force = 2.0 * delta_coherence * dt
   206→        evo.coherence += restoring_force
   207→
   208→        # If coherence strays too far from 0.5, the state vector rapidly decays
   209→        if abs(evo.coherence - 0.5) > 0.4:
   210→            vec *= np.exp(-dt * 5.0)
   211→
   212→        return evo, vec
   213→
   214→
   215→class SacredGeometryEngine:
   216→    """Maps latent states to Sacred Geometry topological invariants."""
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/universe/truth_anchor.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Adversarial Reality Check Bridge (Story 1-0-7).\n\nInjects immutable physics constants (Truth Anchors) into the 12D manifold\nto detect and pop Coherence Bubbles — shared agentic hallucinations where\na swarm reaches internal consensus that violates physical constraints.\n\nThe key insight: a healthy swarm has *variance* in coherence (agents explore\ndifferent regions of the manifold). A swarm where all agents lock to the\nsame coherence value far from 0.5 is exhibiting groupthink, not stability.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom cohezion.universe.hiho_unified_engine import EvoState, HIHOStabilizationEngine\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n# Physics constants (immutable truth anchors)\nHIHO_SPRING_CONSTANT = 2.0\nHIHO_COHERENCE_TARGET = 0.5\nHIHO_MAX_DRIFT = 0.4\n\n# Bubble detection thresholds\nBUBBLE_MEAN_DRIFT_THRESHOLD = 0.25  # Mean coherence > this far from 0.5\nBUBBLE_LOW_VARIANCE_THRESHOLD = 0.005  # Suspiciously identical coherence values\nMIN_SWARM_SIZE_FOR_BUBBLE = 3  # Need at least 3 EVOs to detect a bubble\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass TruthAnchor:\n    \"\"\"An immutable physics constant used to validate swarm behavior.\"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    expected_value: float\n    tolerance: float = 0.01\n    description: str = \"\"\n\n    @classmethod\n    def hiho_spring_constant(cls) -> TruthAnchor:\n        return cls(\n            name=\"hiho_spring_constant\",\n            expected_value=HIHO_SPRING_CONSTANT,\n            tolerance=0.001,\n            description=\"HIHO restoring force coefficient (Hooke's Law analog)\",\n        )\n\n    @classmethod\n    def coherence_target(cls) -> TruthAnchor:\n        return cls(\n            name=\"coherence_target\",\n            expected_value=HIHO_COHERENCE_TARGET,\n            tolerance=BUBBLE_MEAN_DRIFT_THRESHOLD,\n            description=\"HIHO equilibrium coherence target\",\n        )\n\n    @classmethod\n    def energy_conservation(cls) -> TruthAnchor:\n        return cls(\n            name=\"energy_conservation\",\n            expected_value=0.0,  # Delta energy should be ~0\n            tolerance=0.1,\n            description=\"Total energy change per tick should be bounded\",\n        )\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass CoherenceBubble:\n    \"\"\"A detected coherence bubble — shared hallucination violating physics.\"\"\"\n\n    anchor_name: str\n    expected: float\n    observed: float\n    severity: float  # 0.0 = mild, 1.0 = critical\n    description: str = \"\"\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass RestoringForceResult:\n    \"\"\"Result of checking HIHO restoring force on an EVO.\"\"\"\n\n    new_coherence: float\n    force_applied: float\n    original_coherence: float\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ValidationResult:\n    \"\"\"Result of a full truth anchor validation pass.\"\"\"\n\n    passed: bool\n    bubbles: list[CoherenceBubble] = field(default_factory=list)\n    anchors_checked: int = 0\n\n\nclass TruthAnchorValidator:\n    \"\"\"Validates swarm state against immutable physics truth anchors.\n\n    Detects coherence bubbles by checking:\n    1. Mean coherence drift from HIHO target (0.5)\n    2. Suspiciously low variance (groupthink indicator)\n    3. HIHO restoring force compliance\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        self._hiho_engine = HIHOStabilizationEngine()\n        self._anchors = [\n            TruthAnchor.coherence_target(),\n            TruthAnchor.hiho_spring_constant(),\n        ]\n\n    def validate(\n        self, evos: list[EvoState], vectors: list[np.ndarray]\n    ) -> ValidationResult:\n        \"\"\"Run all truth anchor checks against the current swarm state.\"\"\"\n        if len(evos) < MIN_SWARM_SIZE_FOR_BUBBLE:\n            return ValidationResult(passed=True, anchors_checked=0)\n\n        bubbles: list[CoherenceBubble] = []\n        coherences = np.array([evo.coherence for evo in evos])\n\n        # Check 1: Mean coherence drift from target\n        mean_coh = float(np.mean(coherences))\n        drift = abs(mean_coh - HIHO_COHERENCE_TARGET)\n        if drift > BUBBLE_MEAN_DRIFT_THRESHOLD:\n            severity = min(drift / HIHO_MAX_DRIFT, 1.0)\n            bubbles.append(\n                CoherenceBubble(\n                    anchor_name=\"coherence_target\",\n                    expected=HIHO_COHERENCE_TARGET,\n                    observed=mean_coh,\n                    severity=severity,\n                    description=f\"Mean coherence {mean_coh:.3f} drifted {drift:.3f} from target\",\n                )\n            )\n\n        # Check 2: Suspiciously low variance (groupthink)\n        if len(coherences) >= MIN_SWARM_SIZE_FOR_BUBBLE:\n            variance = float(np.var(coherences))\n            if variance < BUBBLE_LOW_VARIANCE_THRESHOLD and drift > 0.1:\n                bubbles.append(\n                    CoherenceBubble(\n                        anchor_name=\"coherence_variance\",\n                        expected=BUBBLE_LOW_VARIANCE_THRESHOLD,\n                        observed=variance,\n                        severity=min(1.0, (0.1 - variance) / 0.1 + drift),\n                        description=(\n                            f\"Zero-variance coherence ({variance:.6f}) at \"\n                            f\"mean={mean_coh:.3f} — possible groupthink\"\n                        ),\n                    )\n                )\n\n        passed = len(bubbles) == 0\n        if not passed:\n            logger.warning(\n                \"Coherence bubble detected: %d violations in %d EVOs\",\n                len(bubbles),\n                len(evos),\n            )\n\n        return ValidationResult(\n            passed=passed,\n            bubbles=bubbles,\n            anchors_checked=len(self._anchors),\n        )\n\n    def check_restoring_force(\n        self, evo: EvoState, dt: float = 0.1\n    ) -> RestoringForceResult:\n        \"\"\"Verify HIHO restoring force behaves as expected for a single EVO.\"\"\"\n        original = evo.coherence\n        dummy_vec = np.zeros(12)\n\n        # Apply one HIHO tick\n        updated_evo, _ = self._hiho_engine.apply_hiho_loop(evo, dummy_vec, dt)\n        force = HIHO_SPRING_CONSTANT * (HIHO_COHERENCE_TARGET - original) * dt\n\n        return RestoringForceResult(\n            new_coherence=updated_evo.coherence,\n            force_applied=force,\n            original_coherence=original,\n        )\n"
}
```

**12 passed, 100% coverage on `truth_anchor.py`.** All green.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Why this implementation is elegantly simple:**
- `TruthAnchor` is a dataclass with factory methods — no inheritance hierarchy, no registry pattern
- `TruthAnchorValidator.validate()` does two statistical checks: mean drift and low variance. That's it. Two `if` statements that detect the two fundamental failure modes of swarm coherence
- The key physics insight: **variance is the signal**. A healthy swarm has agents exploring different coherence values. When variance drops to zero AND mean drifts from 0.5, that's a coherence bubble — agents are hallucinating together
- `check_restoring_force()` reuses the existing `HIHOStabilizationEngine` rather than reimplementing physics
- 72 lines of production code, 100% test coverage, 12 tests
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

```
715:### Story 3.3: Metacognitive Intent Capture (Black Box)
716-As a Research Engineer,
717-I want every agent to log a JSON payload explaining its 512D intent,
718-So that every physical state change is grounded in semantic reasoning.
719-
720-**Traces:** [FR-6]
721-
722-**Acceptance Criteria:**
723-
724-**Given** an active agent in the swarm
725-**When** the agent proposes a 12D physical state change
726-**Then** it must log a JSON payload including the 512D latent vector and human-readable intent.
727-
728-**Given** an agent fails to produce a valid intent payload (malformed JSON or missing 512D vector)
729-**When** the intent capture middleware intercepts the request
730-**Then** the state change is blocked and the event is logged as a "Silent Intent Violation" for Ouroboros training
731-**And** the blocking agent is flagged for Triune review before its next action.
732-
733-**Development Protocol** (see [development-standards.md](development-standards.md)):
734-- [ ] TDD: Red (failing test per AC) -> Green (minimal code) -> Refactor
735-- [ ] Coverage: >= 90% line, >= 80% branch (business logic); contract tests for boundaries
--
1060:### Story 5.1: Ouroboros Trigger (VAE Fine-Tuning Loop)
1061-As a Research Engineer,
1062-I want TDD failures to automatically trigger a VAE fine-tuning iteration,
1063-So that the system autonomously heals its own drift.
1064-
1065-**Traces:** [NFR-4, NFR-8]
1066-
1067-**Acceptance Criteria:**
1068-
1069-**Given** a TDD "Red" state
1070-**When** the Triune Consensus confirms the hash
1071-**Then** the system initiates a VAE fine-tuning iteration.
1072-
1073-**Given** the VAE fine-tuning iteration diverges (loss increases for 3+ consecutive epochs)
1074-**When** the Ouroboros watchdog detects divergence
1075-**Then** the iteration is halted, the checkpoint is rolled back to the last stable state, and the divergence event is logged as a Freeze-Frame for post-mortem analysis
1076-**And** the system does not deploy a degraded encoder.
1077-
1078-**Given** no Triune Consensus is reached (agents disagree on the failure hash)
1079-**When** the consensus timeout expires (30 seconds)
1080-**Then** the failure is escalated to the Vault as an unresolved Experiment and the fine-tuning is deferred
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/src/cohezion/universe/intent_capture.py",
  "content": "\"\"\"Metacognitive Intent Capture middleware (Story 3.3, FR6).\n\nEvery agent must log a JSON payload explaining its intent before executing\na 12D physical state change. This creates an auditable trail of agent\nreasoning and blocks \"silent\" state mutations.\n\nViolations are captured as training data for the Ouroboros fine-tuning loop,\nenabling the system to learn from its own intent-action misalignments.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport logging\nimport time\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n# Axiomatic manifold dimension\nMANIFOLD_DIM = 12\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass IntentPayload:\n    \"\"\"JSON payload an agent must provide before a state change.\"\"\"\n\n    agent_id: str\n    intent: str  # Human-readable reasoning\n    latent_vector: list[float]  # 12D axiomatic vector\n\n    def is_valid(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Check structural validity of the intent payload.\"\"\"\n        if not self.agent_id or not self.agent_id.strip():\n            return False\n        if not self.intent or not self.intent.strip():\n            return False\n        if len(self.latent_vector) != MANIFOLD_DIM:\n            return False\n        return True\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict:\n        \"\"\"Serialize for audit logging.\"\"\"\n        return {\n            \"agent_id\": self.agent_id,\n            \"intent\": self.intent,\n            \"latent_vector\": self.latent_vector,\n        }\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass IntentViolation:\n    \"\"\"A blocked state change due to missing or invalid intent.\"\"\"\n\n    violation_type: str  # \"missing_intent\" | \"invalid_intent\"\n    agent_id: str | None\n    timestamp: float = field(default_factory=time.time)\n    details: str = \"\"\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass StateChangeRequest:\n    \"\"\"A proposed 12D state change with optional intent payload.\"\"\"\n\n    intent: IntentPayload | None\n    proposed_state: np.ndarray\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass CheckResult:\n    \"\"\"Result of intent capture check.\"\"\"\n\n    approved: bool\n    violation: IntentViolation | None = None\n\n\nclass IntentCapture:\n    \"\"\"Middleware that validates intent before allowing state changes.\n\n    Blocks state changes that lack valid intent payloads and accumulates\n    violations as training data for the Ouroboros fine-tuning loop.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        self._violations: list[IntentViolation] = []\n\n    @property\n    def violations(self) -> list[IntentViolation]:\n        return list(self._violations)\n\n    def check(self, request: StateChangeRequest) -> CheckResult:\n        \"\"\"Validate a state change request against intent requirements.\"\"\"\n        if request.intent is None:\n            violation = IntentViolation(\n                violation_type=\"missing_intent\",\n                agent_id=None,\n                details=\"State change proposed without intent payload\",\n            )\n            self._violations.append(violation)\n            logger.warning(\"Silent Intent Violation: %s\", violation.details)\n            return CheckResult(approved=False, violation=violation)\n\n        if not request.intent.is_valid():\n            violation = IntentViolation(\n                violation_type=\"invalid_intent\",\n                agent_id=request.intent.agent_id or None,\n                details=\"Malformed intent payload (missing fields or wrong vector dimension)\",\n            )\n            self._violations.append(violation)\n            logger.warning(\n                \"Invalid Intent Violation from %s: %s\",\n                request.intent.agent_id,\n                violation.details,\n            )\n            return CheckResult(approved=False, violation=violation)\n\n        logger.debug(\n            \"Intent approved for %s: %s\",\n            request.intent.agent_id,\n            request.intent.intent[:80],\n        )\n        return CheckResult(approved=True)\n\n    def get_training_data(self) -> list[dict]:\n        \"\"\"Export violations as training data for Ouroboros.\"\"\"\n        return [\n            {\n                \"violation_type\": v.violation_type,\n                \"agent_id\": v.agent_id,\n                \"timestamp\": v.timestamp,\n                \"details\": v.details,\n            }\n            for v in self._violations\n        ]\n\n    def clear(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Clear violation history after Ouroboros consumption.\"\"\"\n        self._violations.clear()\n"
}
```
